### Notes

[^486]: Typikon Appendix (Supplement): Title Page. Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p853 (printed page 838) and facing English leaf p854 (printed page 839). Half-title leaf introducing the Appendix to the 1888 Violakis Typikon: Greek: ΠΑΡΑΡΤΗΜΑ ΤΟΥ ΤΥΠΙΚΟΥ; English: TYPIKON APPENDIX. The Appendix contains special ceremonial orders of the Great Church of Christ (Patriarchate of Constantinople), beginning with the solemn Hierarchical Service of the Akathist Hymn on Friday of the Fifth Week of Great Lent.
[^487]: Order of Service of the Akathist Hymn in the Patriarchal Church: Opening Rubrics (§ Appendix 1). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p855 (printed page 840) and facing English leaf p856 (printed page 841). Ceremony of the Akathist Hymn on the Friday evening of the Fifth Week of the Great Fast: At the Ninth Hour [3:00 p.m.], the Ecumenical Patriarch descends with the customary patriarchal entourage into the Narthex, where he vests with the Mandyas [Hierarchical Mantle] and receives the Pastoral Staff (*Pateritsa*). He enters the Nave, blesses the congregation, and ascends the Patriarchal Throne. While the Choirs sing "For many years, Master!" (*Εἰς πολλὰ ἔτη, Δέσποτα*), the Great Archimandrite, the Great Archdeacon, and the Second Deacon receive Keron (*καιρόν*)—the formal liturgical blessing and permission to officiate from the Patriarch. The Great Archimandrite intones the opening Exclamation: "Blessed is our God always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen." (*Εὐλογητὸς ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν*), and the Patriarch begins: "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee!", "O Heavenly King, Comforter, Spirit of truth...". The designated Reader-Priest, standing opposite the Patriarch near the Primikirios, reads the Trisagion Prayers and Small Compline. Following "It is truly right" (*Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*), the First Choir chants the Troparion in Tone 8: "Taking knowledge of the secret command" (*Τὸ προσταχθὲν μυστικῶς*) slowly and melodiously. During this hymn, the officiating Hierarchs exit the Bema through the South Deacon's Gate, led by the Great Ecclesiarch, and take their places in the Soleas according to seniority. [Printed Note 1: Receiving Keron (*καιρόν*) is the formal blessing of the highest-ranking hierarch authorizing clergy to participate in the sacred office. Printed Note 2: Patriarchal court offices (*offikia*) such as Primikirios and Megas Logothetis originated in Byzantine administrative governance and became honorary ceremonial titles in the post-Byzantine Great Church of Christ].
[^488]: Order of Service of the Akathist Hymn in the Patriarchal Church: First Stasis and Opening Odes (§ Appendix 2). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p857 (printed page 842) and facing English leaf p858 (printed page 843). The Great Ecclesiarch, having made a reverent bow (*σχῆμα*) to the Patriarch, returns to the Bema with the Great Archdeacon and Second Deacon. As the Second Choir repeats "Taking knowledge of the secret command" (*Τὸ προσταχθὲν μυστικῶς*), the vested Deacons with Dikerotrikera, led by the Ecclesiarch, exit the Beautiful Gate and flank the Patriarchal Throne. Midway through the hymn, the Patriarch descends, enters the Sanctuary preceded by the Primikirios, Ecclesiarch, and Deacons, kisses the Holy Table, and is vested with Epitrachilion and Omophorion. The Primikirios stands before the Festal Icon of the Theotokos on the Soleas. As the First Choir chants "Taking knowledge..." for the third time at a moderate tempo, the Patriarch exits the Sanctuary, censes the Holy Icon three times, and solemnly intones the First Stasis of the Oikoi (Oikoi 1–6: A–Z / Ἀ–Ζ), censing three times at the end of each Ikos. He then kisses the Icon, blesses the people with the Trikirion while the Choirs sing "For many years, Master!", and returns to the Sanctuary to unvest from the Epitrachilion and Omophorion before re-ascending his Throne. The Choirs sing Odes 1 and 3 of the Canon ("I shall open my mouth and it shall be filled with the Spirit" / *Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου*), followed by the slow, solemn singing of the Kontakion in Tone 8: "To the Champion General" (*Τῇ ὑπερμάχῳ*) by both Choirs. During its penultimate verse, the Great Ecclesiarch invites the first officiating Hierarch to enter the Bema. [Printed Note 3: The reverent sign (*σχῆμα*) consists of placing the right hand over the chest and bowing slightly. Printed Note 4: The Dikerion symbolizes the two natures of Christ (divine and human), and the Trikirion symbolizes the three Persons of the Holy Trinity].
[^489]: Order of Service of the Akathist Hymn in the Patriarchal Church: Stases 2–4, Canon Odes 4–9, and Divamvulon (§ Appendix 3). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p859 (printed page 844) and facing English leaf p860 (printed page 845). The first officiating Hierarch kisses the Patriarch's hand, enters the Sanctuary preceded by the Ecclesiarch, and vests with Epitrachilion and Omophorion. Preceded by the Third Deacon and another Deacon with Dikerotrikera, he exits to the Festal Icon, censes the Patriarch and the Icon three times each, and intones the Second Stasis of the Oikoi (Oikoi 7–12: H–M / Η–Μ). Upon conclusion, he kisses the Icon, bows to the Patriarch, returns to the Bema, unvests, and remains inside. The Choirs sing Odes 4, 5, and 6 of the Canon. During Ode 6, the Ecclesiarch invites the second Hierarch, who receives Keron from the Patriarch, vests in the Bema, and towards the end of the fast "To the Champion General" (*Τῇ ὑπερμάχῳ*), exits with the Deacons to intone the Third Stasis of the Oikoi (Oikoi 13–18: N–P / Ν–Π). He unvests in the Bema. The Choirs sing Odes 7, 8, and 9 of the Canon; during Ode 7, the two vested Deacons cense the congregation. During Ode 9, the third Hierarch receives Keron, vests, exits towards the conclusion of "To the Champion General", and intones the Fourth Stasis of the Oikoi (Oikoi 19–24: T–Omega / Τ–Ω, concluding with the repetition of Ikos 1). The remaining Hierarchs in attendance come before the Patriarch in order of seniority, bow, kiss the Festal Icon, and enter the Bema. Following the final rapid singing of "To the Champion General", the Reader recites the Trisagion and Kontakion, and the Primikirios comes forward with the Divamvulon (a large double-branched candlestick) and stands opposite the Patriarchal Throne.
[^490]: Service of the Akathist Hymn Concluded (§ Appendix 4). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p861 (printed page 846) and facing English leaf p862 (printed page 847). Concluding rubrics of the solemn Patriarchal Service of the Akathist Hymn: Following the Fourth Stasis and the entrance with the Divamvulon, the Great Ecclesiarch, Priests, and Deacons take their stations across from and beside the Patriarchal Throne. The Reader-Priest recites "To the Champion General" (*Τῇ ὑπερμάχῳ*), the Forty Lord Have Mercies, and the Prayer of the Canonical Hours: "Thou Who at all times and at every hour..." (*Ὁ ἐν παντὶ καιρῷ*). The Patriarch recites "May God be merciful unto us and bless us..." (*Ὁ Θεὸς οἰκτιρήσαι ἡμᾶς*), and the Canonarchs chant the Compline prayers: "O Spotless, Undefiled, Incorrupt..." (*Ἄσπιλε, ἀμόλυντε*) and "And grant unto us, O Master..." (*Καὶ δὸς ἡμῖν, Δέσποτα*). The Priest intones the Small Dismissal and the Fervent Supplication for the peace of the world (*Εὐξώμεθα ὑπὲρ εἰρήνης τοῦ κόσμου*). The Choirs sing the slow Kathisma in Tone 3: "Seeing the beauty of thy virginity..." (*Τὴν ὡραιότητα τῆς παρθενίας σου*), during which the Patriarch descends from the Throne, blesses the congregation, and departs, while the Priest concludes with the solemn Patriarchal Dismissal: "Through the prayers of our holy Master, Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen." (*Δι' εὐχῶν τοῦ ἁγίου Δεσπότου ἡμῶν...*). [Printed Note: At the Dismissal, when the Patriarch himself concludes or prior to the priest's exclamation, the Patriarch proclaims: "Through the prayers of our Holy Fathers, Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen."].
[^491]: Order of the Service of the Holy Passion in the Patriarchal Church: Procession, Vesting, and Entourage (§ Appendix 5). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p863 (printed page 848) and facing English leaf p864 (printed page 849). Patriarchal ceremonial for the Twelve Passion Gospels (*Τῶν Ἁγίων Παθῶν*) on Holy and Great Thursday evening: Bells peal at the Twelfth Hour [6:00 p.m.]. His All-Holiness the Ecumenical Patriarch descends with his entourage half an hour later. The procession is led by four staff-bearing ushers (*κλητῆρες ῥαβδοῦχοι*) proclaiming the Patriarch's arrival, followed by all parish clergy and the Great Archimandrite, and the Great Ecclesiarch bearing the pastoral staff (*πατριαρχικὴ ποιμαντικὴ ῥάβδος* / *Pateritsa*). The Patriarch walks bearing a plain, unadorned walking staff (*χαζράνιον* / *Chazranion*), flanked by the Great Protosynkellos on his right and the Great Archdeacon on his left, followed by the junior deacons. At the Great Gate of the church, the Patriarch is vested with the Mandyas by the Great Archdeacon, and the pastoral staff is ceremonially transferred to him by the Protosynkellos. The procession enters the nave led by the Great Primikirios carrying the large double-branched candlestick (*διβάμβουλον* / *Divamvulon*). In the center of the nave, the Patriarch blesses the congregation while the Choirs sing "For many years, Master!" (*Εἰς πολλὰ ἔτη, Δέσποτα*). [Printed Note: The *Chazranion* is a plain cane devoid of ornamentation, uniquely carried during the solemnity of Great and Holy Week].
[^492]: Order of the Service of the Holy Passion: Opening Rubrics, Hexapsalmos, and Troparion (§ Appendix 6). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p865 (printed page 850) and facing English leaf p866 (printed page 851). Sacerdotal action and introductory Matins order for the Twelve Gospels: The Patriarch ascends the Throne; the Great Archimandrite, Great Archdeacon, and Second Deacon receive Keron (*καιρόν*), followed by the junior deacons. The Great Archimandrite, vested in Epitrachilion at the Holy Table, intones the opening blessing: "Blessed is our God always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen." (*Εὐλογητὸς ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν*), and the Patriarch recites the Trisagion Prayers. The Reader-Priest reads the introductory Royal Psalms (Psalms 19 and 20 LXX), during which the Archimandrite, vested in Phelonion and preceded by a candle-bearer, censes the whole church using a hand censer (*Κατζί* / *Katzion*). Following the Trisagion exclamation and customary royal troparia, the Archimandrite intones the augmented petition and exclamation: "Glory to the holy, consubstantial, life-creating, and undivided Trinity, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.", whereupon the Patriarch recites the Six Psalms (*Ἑξάψαλμος*). After the Great Litany, the Choirs chant "Alleluia" four times with the appointed verses from Isaiah 26:9, 11, 15 (LXX), followed by the Troparion in Tone 8: "When the glorious disciples were enlightened at the washing of the feet..." (*Ὅτε οἱ ἔνδοξοι μαθηταί*), sung twice slowly and once moderately fast (*ἀργοσυντόμως*). During this hymn, the concelebrating Hierarchs exit the Sanctuary led by the Great Ecclesiarch to take their stations, and the Archdeacon enters the Bema to vest. [Editorial Note: Violakis accidentally transposed the third and fourth verses of the Alleluia in the Greek text].
[^493]: Order of the Service of the Holy Passion: Gospels 1 and 2, Antiphons, and Kathismata (§ Appendix 7). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p867 (printed page 852) and facing English leaf p868 (printed page 853). Proclamation of the Passion Gospels and hierarchical rotations: The vested Deacons exit the Royal Doors holding Dikerotrikera and flank the Patriarchal Throne. As "When the glorious disciples..." is sung for the third time, the Patriarch descends, enters the Sanctuary, vests with Epitrachilion and Omophorion, and the Holy Gospel is placed upon the Triskelion before the Royal Doors. Following the liturgical dialogue intoned by the Great Archdeacon, the Patriarch proclaims the First Passion Gospel (John 13:31–18:1). After blessing the faithful with the Trikirion, the Patriarch unvests in the Bema and returns to his Throne. The Choirs sing the 15 Antiphons of Holy Friday Matins. During the Second Antiphon, the Great Ecclesiarch invites the senior Hierarch, who receives Keron from the Patriarch, vests in the Bema, and upon the conclusion of the Kathisma in Tone 7 ("While feeding the disciples at the Supper..."), reads the Second Passion Gospel (John 18:1–28) at the Triskelion. At each Gospel reading, the Choirs alternate singing rapidly: "Glory to Thy forbearance, O Lord, glory to Thee!" (*Δόξα τῇ μακροθυμίᾳ σου, Κύριε, δόξα σοι*).
[^494]: Order of the Service of the Holy Passion: Gospels 3–6, Antiphons 4–15, and the Procession of the Crucified (§ Appendix 8). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p869 (printed page 854) and facing English leaf p870 (printed page 855). Succession of Gospel readings and the dramatic Solemn Procession of the Crucified Cross: Officiating Hierarchs read successive Gospels in order of seniority; remaining Gospels are proclaimed by the Great Protosynkellos, Grand Chancellor-Secretary (*Ἀρχιγραμματεύς*), Great Archimandrite, and Synkellos. After Gospel 2, Choir 2 sings Antiphons 4–6 and Kathisma in Tone 7 ("What manner of way, O Judas..."), followed by Gospel 3 (Matt. 26:57–75). Choir 1 sings Antiphons 7–9 and Kathisma in Tone 8 ("Oh, how did Judas..."), followed by Gospel 4 (John 18:28–19:16). Choir 2 sings Antiphons 10–12 and Kathisma in Tone 8 ("When Thou, O God, stoodest before Caiaphas..."), followed by Gospel 5 (Matt. 27:3–32). Choir 1 sings Antiphons 13–15 through the Theotokion ("Rejoice, thou who through the Angel..."). The Great Archimandrite, vested in Epitrachilion and Phelonion, lifts the Crucifix (*Ἐσταυρωμένος*), circumambulates the Holy Table, and intones in a sorrowful recitative the Idiomelon in Tone 6 [Tone 2 Plagal]: "Today He Who suspended the earth upon the waters is suspended upon the Tree!" (*Σήμερον κρεμᾶται ἐπὶ ξύλου*). Preceded by the Primikirios, candle-bearers, and censing Deacons, he carries the Cross into the center of the Nave and erects it in its floor socket. Choir 1 repeats the Idiomelon, and Choir 2 sings the concluding Stichera of Antiphon 15 and Kathisma in Tone 4 ("Thou hast redeemed us from the curse of the Law..."), leading into Gospel 6 (Mark 15:16–32) and the Beatitudes. [Printed Note 1: Historical note by George Violakis noting that the Procession of the Crucified was introduced into the Patriarchal Church under Ecumenical Patriarch Sophronios IV (1864) and had not existed in earlier printed Triodia or ancient Typika, but was sanctioned to meet the intense piety of the faithful].
[^495]: Service of the Holy Passion Concluded: Gospels 7–11, Canon, and Praises (§ Appendix 9). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p871 (printed page 856) and facing English leaf p872 (printed page 857). Sacerdotal action and liturgical order of the Passion Gospels: Following Gospel 6 and the Beatitudes with their Troparia, the Prokeimenon in Tone 4 ("They parted My garments among them...") is chanted and Gospel 7 (Matt. 27:33–54) is read. The Patriarch recites Psalm 50 LXX recto tono, followed by Gospel 8 (Luke 23:32–49). Choir 1 chants the Triodion Canon (Kosmas of Maiuma, Ode 5: "Unto Thee I rise early at dawn..."), with four Troparia per Ode with the refrain "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee!". Following Ode 5, the Small Litany is intoned, and the Kontakion in Tone 8 ("Come, let us all sing the praise..."), Ikos, Menologion, and Great Friday Synaxarion are read. Choir 2 sings Ode 8, the Deacon exclaims "The Theotokos and Mother of the Light...", and the Patriarch intones the Magnifications of Ode 9 ("More honorable than the Cherubim..."). Following the Small Litany, the Exapostilarion ("The good thief didst Thou account worthy of Paradise...") is sung three times, during which the Primikirios takes his station. Gospel 9 (John 19:25–37) is read, followed by the Praises ("Let every breath..."). At the verse "Praise Him, all His angels...", the Patriarch descends from the Throne, bows and venerates the Crucifix, blesses the congregation while Choir 1 sings "For many years, Master!", and returns to the Throne. The Choirs sing the 4 Idiomela of the Praises, followed by the Doxastikon ("I gave My back to scourgings...") and Gospel 10 (Mark 15:43–47). The Patriarch recites the Small Doxology recto tono, the Great Archimandrite intones the Morning Litany, and Gospel 11 (John 19:38–42) is read. As the Aposticha begin, the Great Archdeacon receives the blessing from the Patriarch, vests in the Bema, and at the Doxastikon ("Already the pen of sentence is dipped in ink...") brings forth the Gospel Book to the Patriarch for the blessing of the proclaimer.
[^496]: Service of the Holy Passion Concluded: Gospel 12, Dismissal, and Patriarchal Acclamation (§ Appendix 10). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p873 (printed page 858) and facing English leaf p874 (printed page 859). Final liturgical rites of the Holy Passion service: The Patriarch blesses the Great Archdeacon: "May God, through the intercessions of the holy and glorious Apostle and Evangelist Matthew...", who ascends the Ambo. At the Royal Doors, the Great Archimandrite exclaims "Wisdom! Stand upright! Let us hear the Holy Gospel!", and the Archdeacon proclaims Gospel 12 (Matt. 27:62–66). After the reading, the Patriarch blesses the congregation while the Choirs sing "For many years, Master!", and the Archdeacon brings the Gospel Book to the Patriarch to venerate. The Patriarch says "It is a good thing to give praise unto the Lord...", the Reader recites the Trisagion Prayers, and the Choirs chant the Dismissal Troparion in Tone 4 ("Thou hast redeemed us from the curse of the Law by Thy precious Blood...") three times. The Archimandrite intones the Augmented Litany and the solemn Dismissal: "He Who endured spitting and scourgings, buffetings and the Cross and death, for our salvation, Christ our true God...". During the Dismissal, the Patriarch blesses the people as the Choirs chant "Our Master and Chief Priest, O Lord, preserve and keep for many years!". The Patriarch descends, signs himself three times, and departs in procession while the Priest exclaims "Through the prayers of our holy Master...". In the Narthex, the Patriarch removes the Mandyas, turns, and blesses as the Great Primikirios proclaims his Polychronion ("May the Lord God grant many years unto our most holy and venerable Master, the Ecumenical Patriarch..."). Ascending to the balcony of the Chancellory (Protosynkellia), the Patriarch blesses the crowds in the courtyard one final time before retiring to his chambers.
[^497]: Order of Service for the Consecration of a Church: Situations 1 and 2 (§ Consecration Rubrics 1–2). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p875 (printed page 860) and facing English leaf p876 (printed page 861). Foundational rubrics for the Consecration (Enkainia) of a Christian Temple: Situation 1 governs the Consecration occurring on the commemoration of a celebrated Saint: At Vespers ("Lord, I have cried"), 10 Stichera: 4 of the Consecration, 3 of the celebrated Saint, and 3 of the Patron Saint of the consecrated church; Glory of the Saint, Now and forever of the Consecration; 3 Old Testament Readings (2 of Consecration, 1 of Saint); Aposticha of the Saint, Glory of the Patron Saint of the church, Now and forever of the Consecration; Dismissal Troparia of the Saint, Patron Saint of the church, and Consecration. At Matins, Kathismata of the Saint and Consecration; Canons of Consecration, Saint, and Patron Saint to 4 each (total 12); Praises: 8 Stichera (2 Consecration, 3 Saint, 3 Patron Saint of church); Glory of Patron Saint of church (unless an Apostle, Evangelist, or Forerunner is celebrated, where the celebrated Saint takes precedence), Now and forever of Consecration; Great Doxology and Dismissal Troparion of Consecration. Situation 2 governs Consecration occurring on a Sunday: At Vespers ("Lord, I have cried"), 10 Stichera: 4 of the Resurrection, 3 of the Consecration, and 3 of the Patron Saint of the church; Glory of Patron Saint of church, Now and forever 1st Dogmatic Theotokion of Tone of week; Readings of Consecration; Resurrection Aposticha, Glory of Patron Saint of church, Now and forever of Consecration.
[^498]: Order of Service for the Consecration of a Church: Situations 2 (Concluded), 3, and 4 (§ Consecration Rubrics 2–4). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p877 (printed page 862) and facing English leaf p878 (printed page 863). Consecration of a Temple on Sundays and Feasts of the Mother of God: Continuation of Situation 2 (Sunday Consecration): At Matins, Resurrection Kathismata with Consecration Kathismata in place of Theotokia; Canons of Resurrection, Consecration, and Patron Saint of church; Praises: 4 Resurrection and 4 Consecration; Glory of Patron Saint of church, Now and forever of Consecration; Great Doxology and "Today salvation is come unto the world...". Situation 3 governs Consecration on a Sunday with a celebrated Saint: At Vespers ("Lord, I have cried"), 10 Stichera: 3 of Resurrection, 2 of Consecration, 3 of Saint, 2 of Patron Saint of church; Glory of Saint, Now and forever 1st Dogmatic Theotokion of Tone of week; 3 Readings (2 of Consecration, 1 of Saint); Resurrection Aposticha, Glory of Patron Saint of church, Now and forever of Consecration; Dismissal Troparia: Resurrection, Saint, Patron Saint of church, and Consecration. At Matins, Canons of Resurrection, Consecration, and Saint; Praises: 8 Stichera (3 Resurrection, 2 Consecration, 3 Saint); Glory of Patron Saint of church, Now and forever of Consecration; Great Doxology and "Today salvation...". Situation 4 governs Consecration on a Feast of the Mother of God (Theotokos): At Vespers ("Lord, I have cried"), 10 Stichera: 4 of Feast, 3 of Consecration, 3 of Patron Saint of church; Glory of Feast, Now and forever of Consecration.
[^499]: Order of Service for the Consecration of a Church: Situations 4 (Concluded), 5, and 6 (§ Consecration Rubrics 4–6). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p879 (printed page 864) and facing English leaf p880 (printed page 865). Consecration of a Temple on Marian, Despotic, and Sunday-Marian Confluences: Continuation of Situation 4 (Marian Feast): Readings: 2 of Feast, 1 of Consecration; Aposticha of Feast, Glory of Patron Saint of church, Now and forever of Feast; Dismissal Troparia: Feast, Consecration, Patron Saint of church, and again Feast. At Matins, Kathismata of Feast, Consecration, and Feast; Canons of Feast and Consecration; Praises: 8 Stichera (4 Feast, 2 Consecration, 2 Patron Saint of church); Glory of Feast, Now and forever of Consecration; Great Doxology and Dismissal Troparion of Feast. Situation 5 governs Consecration on a Feast of the Lord (Despotic Feast): At Vespers ("Lord, I have cried"), 8 Stichera: 6 of Feast, 2 of Consecration; Glory of Feast, Now and forever of Consecration; 3 Readings (2 Feast, 1 Consecration); Aposticha of Feast, Glory of Patron Saint of church, Now and forever of Feast; Dismissal Troparia: Feast, Consecration, Patron Saint of church, and again Feast. Matins follows the same 3-Kathisma and dual Canon structure; Praises: 8 Stichera (4 Feast, 2 Consecration, 2 Patron Saint of church); Glory of Feast, Now and forever of Consecration. Situation 6 governs Consecration on a Sunday concurring with a Feast of the Mother of God: At Vespers ("Lord, I have cried"), 10 Stichera: 4 of Resurrection, 4 of Feast, 2 of Consecration; Glory of Feast, Now and forever of Consecration; 3 Readings (2 Feast, 1 Consecration); Resurrection Aposticha, Glory of Patron Saint of church, Now and forever of Feast; Dismissal Troparia: Resurrection, Feast, Patron Saint of church, Consecration. Matins commences with Resurrection Kathismata and Consecration Kathismata.
[^500]: Order of Service for the Consecration of a Church Concluded (Case 6) and The Ceremony of Consecration: Preliminary Preparations and Pre-Vesperal Rites (§ Ceremony of Consecration 1). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p881 (printed page 866) and facing English leaf p882 (printed page 867). Conclusion of Case 6 (Sunday Consecration coinciding with a Feast of the Mother of God): At the Praises, 8 Stichera are chanted: 3 of the Resurrection, 3 of the Feast, and 2 of the Patron Saint of the church; Glory of the Feast, Now and forever of the Consecration; Great Doxology, and the Sunday Dismissal Troparion "Today salvation is come unto the world...". The Lectionary readings of the Apostolos and Gospel in each of the six preceding cases are determined and appointed by the presiding Hierarch according to the feast at hand. The rubrics for the solemn Ceremony of Consecration then commence: The presiding Hierarch prepares the necessary liturgical vessels and realia prescribed in the Euchologion. On the eve of the dedication, prior to Vespers, the Bishop with the clergy enters the new church and arranges the Holy Relics in three portions upon the Holy Table. Placed upon the Diskarion (Paten) beneath the Asterisk and covered with the Veil, the Bishop gives the blessing "Blessed is our God", followed by the Trisagion Prayers and the Troparia of the Holy Martyrs ("Your Martyrs, O Lord...", "Champions of the Lord...", "Christ's Martyrs...", and the Troparion of the day), followed by the Kondakion ("As first fruits of nature..."), Glory ("Godlike choir of Martyrs..."), Now and forever ("Theotokos, thou art the true vine, together with the Champions..."), and the Dismissal. If an already consecrated church is located nearby, the Bishop translates the Holy Relics thither before Vespers, placing them on the Holy Table before an all-night candle; Vespers and an All-night Vigil are celebrated there. If no consecrated church is near, the entire vigil is kept in the new church. On the morning of Consecration, Matins is chanted according to the established order. Following the Praises, the Bishop descends from the Throne into the nave, kneels facing East, and recites the two preparatory prayers of Patriarch Kallistos I.
[^501]: The Ceremony of the Consecration: Vesting, Wax-Mastic Preparation, and Procession of Relics (§ Ceremony of Consecration 2). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p883 (printed page 868) and facing English leaf p884 (printed page 869). Archieratical preparation and the external procession: The Bishop completes the two preparatory prayers of Patriarch Kallistos, venerates the holy icons, and vests in his sacred archieratical vestments for the Divine Liturgy while the Choirs chant "Our Master and Chief Priest", followed by the Stichera of the Praises, Doxastikon, Great Doxology, Dismissal Troparion, and Dismissal. The ingredients of the wax-mastic (Kêromastichê) are placed into a cauldron over the fire to melt, and kept near the flame so the mixture does not congeal; cauldrons of water are heated for washing the Holy Table. Heavy paper strips are bound around the rim of the central Column (which supports the Holy Table, symbolizing Christ, while the four surrounding corner pillars symbolize the Four Evangelists), protruding two fingers above the upper lip and filled with pulverized marble to retain the molten wax-mastic. With all preparations completed, the Bishop intones "Blessed is our God", Psalm 142 LXX is recited, followed by the Small Litany intoned by the Deacon. The Bishop exclaims "For holy art Thou, our God...", the Deacon intones "Let us pray to the Lord", the Bishop recites the Prayer and gives "Peace be unto all", the Deacon intones "Let us bow our heads unto the Lord", and the Bishop recites the secret Prayer and exclaims "Blessed and glorified be the majesty of Thy kingdom". The Bishop then takes the Diskarion with the Holy Relics upon his head, preceded by Priests carrying the Holy Gospel Books, the Cross, Ripidia (Exapteryga), and Candles, while the Choirs sing the Troparia "O Holy Martyrs..." and "Glory to Thee, O Christ God...". The Bishop and the entire congregation go forth in solemn procession circling the exterior of the church, as the Chanters chant the Idiomela "Be renewed, be renewed..." and "Of old when renewing...".
[^502]: The Ceremony of the Consecration: Triple Circuit, Entrance Rites, and Deposition of Relics in the Column (§ Ceremony of Consecration 3). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p885 (printed page 870) and facing English leaf p886 (printed page 871). The triple procession, ceremonial portal dialogue, and deposition of relics: Chanting of the processional Idiomela concludes with Glory, Now and forever: "He Who is in the bosom of the Father...". Upon reaching the western portals of the church, the Bishop deposits the Holy Relics upon the Tetrapod; the Deacon proclaims the Apostolos and the Bishop proclaims the Holy Gospel. A second procession circuits the church while chanting Ode 3 of the Consecration Canon, followed by the second readings of Apostolos and Gospel at the portals. A third circuit is made while chanting Ode 6 of the Consecration Canon. Arriving before the closed western portal for the third time, the Bishop exclaims "Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God, unto the ages of ages. Amen", and the Troparion "Upon the rock of faith..." is sung. The Bishop replaces the Relics upon the Tetrapod and recites the Prayer "O God and Father..." followed by the Prayer of the Entrance. With the doors shut, the Bishop enacts the liturgical dialogue of Psalm 23:7–10 LXX: "Lift up your gates, ye princes, and be ye lifted up, O eternal gates, and the King of glory shall enter in!" A cleric within the nave questions: "Who is this King of glory?", to which the Bishop responds: "The Lord strong and mighty, the Lord mighty in battle" (repeated thrice). The Bishop signs the portal cruciformly three times with the Holy Relics; the doors swing open, and the Bishop enters with clergy and people singing the Dismissal Troparion "As the splendor of the firmament on high...". Entering the Holy Sanctuary (Bema), the Bishop deposits the Relics in a silver reliquary box, anoints them with Holy Myron, and secures them with meticulous care, exclaiming thrice: "May the memory of the founders of this holy church be eternal!", to which the people respond: "Eternal memory!". The Bishop recites two prayers, and molten wax-mastic is poured into the central column cavity ("the plant") to seal the Relics. The marble Holy Table slab is positioned upon the Column while the Choirs chant Psalm 144 LXX in Tone 2: "I will exalt Thee, my God...".
[^503]: The Ceremony of the Consecration: Savvanon Vesting, Invocatory Prayers, and Washing of the Holy Table (§ Ceremony of Consecration 4). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p887 (printed page 872) and facing English leaf p888 (printed page 873). The sacred ablution of the Holy Table: While excess wax-mastic is scraped away and the stone cleaned, the Choirs chant Psalm 22 LXX: "The Lord is my shepherd...". The Bishop exclaims "Blessed is our God", and the Savvanon (a white linen garment covering the sacred archieratical vestments front and back) is placed over the Bishop and bound with a belt, and both arms are wrapped in two new white towels (mandylia) tied with a cord. A prayer mat (epeuchion) is set before the Royal Doors. The Deacon intones "Again and again on bended knees, let us pray to the Lord", the Bishop kneels and recites the Prayer "O God without beginning...", followed by the Deacon's suffrages ("Help us, save us...") and the Great Litany. After the Exclamation, the Bishop approaches the Holy Table, takes pieces of scented soap (nitra / moschosapona), signs them cruciformly, and places them upon the slab in the form of a Cross. Warm water is brought, the Bishop bows and blesses it in secret, and following the Exclamation pours it upon the Table saying: "In the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit. Amen." With the warm water and soap, the Bishop and clergy wash the Holy Table and dry it with new natural sponges, cleansing the central Column likewise, while the Choirs chant Psalm 83 LXX: "How lovely are Thy tabernacles, O Lord of hosts...". Following the ablution and drying, the Bishop exclaims "Glory to our God unto the ages.".
[^504]: The Ceremony of the Consecration: Rosewater, Chrismation with Holy Myron, Katasarkion, and Consecration of Antimensia (§ Ceremony of Consecration 5). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p889 (printed page 874) and facing English leaf p890 (printed page 875). Final chrismation, investiture, and Antimensia consecration: The Bishop takes a flagon (vikion / kanion) of rosewater, pours it upon the Holy Table, washes it, and sponges it with the Antimensia, while the Choirs chant Psalm 50:9 LXX in Tone 7 (Grave Tone): "Thou shalt sprinkle me with hyssop, and I shall be cleansed; Thou shalt wash me, and I shall be made whiter than snow.". The Bishop gives glory: "Blessed is our God, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.", and takes the vessel of Holy Myron. At the Deacon's proclamation "Let us attend!", the Bishop pours Holy Myron upon the Holy Table, chanting "Alleluia" three times as at Holy Baptism, traces three Crosses (one central, two lateral), and anoints the entire slab and the Column with his hands, wiping them with the Antimensia. The Choirs chant Psalm 132 LXX: "Behold now, what is so good or so pleasant...", after which the Bishop exclaims the Doxology and "Glory to Thee, O Holy Trinity our God, glory to Thee unto the ages of ages. Amen.". Pieces of linen imprinted with the images or names of the Four Evangelists are affixed to the four corners of the Table with wax-mastic. The Katasarkion (white linen under-vestment) is unfolded over the Holy Table and its cords are tied cruciformly beneath it around the Column while Psalm 131 LXX: "Remember, O Lord, David, and all his meekness..." is sung. The Bishop exclaims "Glory to God unto the ages. Amen.", washes his hands in a clean basin or the sacrarium (choneuterion) so not a single drop is lost, and dries them with a clean towel. He then spreads the outer vestment (Indytion / Ependytes) over the Holy Table while Psalm 92 LXX: "The Lord is King, He is robed in majesty..." is chanted, and proclaims "Glory to Thee, O Holy Trinity our God, unto the ages of ages. Amen.". Finally, the Bishop unfolds the consecrated Antimensia upon the Holy Table one atop another, and upon them the Antimension of the new church.
[^505]: The Ceremony of the Consecration Concluded: Censing, Anointing of the Pillars, Altar Lamp, and Dismissal (§ Ceremony of Consecration 6). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p891 (printed page 876) and facing English leaf p892 (printed page 877). Completion of the altar investiture and consecration rites: Following the deposition of the Antimensia, the Holy Gospel Book is placed upon the Holy Table, and the sacred Artophorion (Tabernacle) is set in its appointed place. The Bishop and clergy venerate the Holy Gospel. The Bishop takes the censer and censes the Holy Table, the sanctuary, and the entire church, while the Choirs chant Psalm 25 LXX: "Judge me, O Lord...". A priest follows the Bishop carrying Holy Myron; with a natural sponge tied to the tip of a reed, he traces the sign of the Cross with Holy Myron upon every column and arch of the church. Upon the completion of the censing and anointing, the Bishop proclaims in a loud voice: "Glory to the holy, all-powerful, and life-creating Trinity, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.". The Deacon intones the Small Litany, the Bishop pronounces the Exclamation: "For unto Thee is due all glory...", followed by the Deacon's suffrage "Let us pray to the Lord", the Bishop's prayer "O Lord our God, Maker of heaven and earth...", the blessing of peace, the prayer of bowing of heads "We thank Thee, O Lord...", and the dismissal suffrage "Let us go forth in peace". The Apostolos is read by the Deacon and the Holy Gospel by the Bishop. A new vigil lamp (kandela) with olive oil and a fresh wick is brought to the Bishop; with his own hands he lights the wick while chanting Tone 1, thrice, the first verse of the Great Doxology: "Glory to God in the highest...", and places the lit lamp upon the Holy Table. The Bishop then divests of the Savvanon (linen apron), the wrist cloths (mandylia), and the belt, which are distributed to the faithful as sanctified tokens. Attendants kindle all the lamps and candles throughout the church while the Doxastikon of the Patron Saint is sung, followed by Now and forever: "While celebrating the commemoration of the Consecration, O Lord...", the Trisagion Prayers, the Dismissal Troparia, the Litany of Completion, Exclamation, and the episcopal Dismissal.
[^506]: The Divine Liturgy for the Consecration of a Church: The Festal Antiphons (§ Consecration Liturgy 1). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p893 (printed page 878) and facing English leaf p894 (printed page 879). Special proper Antiphons appointed for the Divine Liturgy of Consecration: The first Antiphon consists of verses from Psalms 25:8, 83:1–2, 83:4, and 83:5 LXX: "O Lord, I have loved the beauty of Thy house...", "How lovely are Thy tabernacles, O Lord of hosts...", "Thine altars, O Lord of hosts, my King and my God!", and "Blessed are they that dwell in Thy house: they will praise Thee unto the ages of ages.", with the refrain "Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, Savior, save us.". The second Antiphon consists of verses from Psalm 64:1, 64:2, 64:4, and 64:5 LXX: "To Thee praise is due, O God, in Zion...", "Hear my prayer: unto Thee all flesh shall come.", "Blessed is the one whom Thou hast chosen and taken to Thyself...", and "Holy is Thy temple, wonderful in righteousness.", with the refrain "Save us, O Son of God, Who didst arise from the dead, who sing to Thee: Alleluia!". The third Antiphon consists of verses from Psalm 83:11, 83:12, and 83:13 LXX: "I have chosen rather to be an abject in the house of my God...", "For the Lord God loves mercy and truth...", and "The Lord will not withhold good things from them that walk in innocence.", with the Dismissal Troparion of the Consecration ("As the splendor of the firmament on high...") sung as the refrain.
[^507]: The Divine Liturgy Concluded and Precedence Rules for Feasts of the Lord (§ Consecration Liturgy 2). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p895 (printed page 880) and facing English leaf p896 (printed page 881). Liturgical propers concluded and rubrical hierarchy: The lectionary pericopes of the Apostolos and the Holy Gospel are appointed according to the occurring day and rank of the service. The Communion Hymn is Psalm 25:8 LXX: "O Lord, I have loved the beauty of Thy house, and the place where Thy glory dwelleth. Alleluia.", followed by the Dismissal. A cardinal typikon note governs concurrence: If the Consecration of a church is celebrated on a Master Feast (Feast of the Lord), the festal propers of the Master Feast take precedence over those of the Consecration, and the Gospel reading and Communion Hymn of the Master Feast are chanted instead of those of the Consecration, preserving the immutable Christological hierarchy of the Byzantine-Ruthenian rite.
[^508]: Ceremony of the Accession of a Patriarch to the Ecumenical Throne: Arrival, Reception, and Supplications (§ Accession of a Patriarch 1). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p897 (printed page 882) and facing English leaf p898 (printed page 883). The ancient Constantinopolitan rite of patriarchal accession (Anarrhesis): The Patriarch arrives in state at the Patriarchate and is received at the Great Portal of the patriarchal cathedral church by the entire assembled clergy. Accompanying Hierarchs enter directly into the Holy Sanctuary (Bema); four deacons vest in sacred vestments, two censing the Patriarch with Dikerotrikera as he enters the nave while two accompany him. Preceded by the Primikirios carrying the imperial double candle (Divamboulon), and with the Choirs chanting "It is truly meet to bless thee, O Theotokos...", the Patriarch enters the Sanctuary. The senior Hierarch by episcopal ordination vests in Epitrachilion and Omophorion and intones "Blessed is our God, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.", followed by the Trisagion Prayers and the Exclamation "For Thine is the kingdom...". The Hierarchs chant the Dismissal Troparion of Pentecost ("Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God...") and the Kontakion of Pentecost ("When the Most High came down..."). The senior Hierarch intones the Ektenia of Fervent Supplication ("Have mercy on us, O God..."), with threefold responses of "Lord, have mercy" from the Hierarchs, supplications for all devout and Orthodox Christians, and the priestly Exclamation.
[^509]: Ceremony of the Accession of a Patriarch: Investiture, Enthronement, Polychronion, and Dismissal (§ Accession of a Patriarch 2). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p899 (printed page 884) and facing English leaf p900 (printed page 885). Solemn investiture, elevation to the throne, and dismissal: Following the Exclamation, the Dismissal of Pentecost is pronounced: "May Christ our true God, Who sent down the All-Holy Spirit from heaven upon His holy disciples and apostles in the form of fiery tongues...", omitting the concluding clause "Through the prayers of our holy fathers...". The Patriarch, vested in Mandya, Epitrachilion, and Omophorion, stands before the Holy Table, intones "Blessed is our God", reads the Trisagion Prayers, and gives the Dismissal. The Patriarch stands at the Beautiful Gate (Royal Doors); the Great Logothete comes forward and with customary proclamation officially invites him to mount the Ecumenical Throne. The Patriarch renders thanks; the Metropolitan of Herakleia approaches, delivers the traditional allocution, bestows the Patriarchal Pastoral Staff upon him, and kisses his right hand. Escorted by the Great Logothete and deacons, His All-Holiness ascends the throne and blesses the people with the Precious Cross, while the Choirs sing "For many years, Master!" (Eis polla eti, Despota). The Protopsaltes (First-Chanter) leads the Choirs in chanting the Patriarchal Polychronion ("May the Lord God preserve for many years our most holy and venerable Master and Lord, the Ecumenical Patriarch..."). A formal allocution is delivered from the Ambo (customarily by the Great Logothete). The Choirs chant "Our Master and Chief Priest, O Lord, preserve for many years!", while the Patriarch blesses the assembly. The clergy in the Sanctuary intone "Through the prayers of our holy Master, Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us and save us. Amen.", concluding the solemn rite of patriarchal accession.
[^510]: Ceremony of the Return of a Patriarch to the Ecumenical Throne: Arrival, Reception, and Supplications (§ Return of a Patriarch 1). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p901 (printed page 886) and facing English leaf p902 (printed page 887). The Constantinopolitan ceremonial order for the reinstallation and return (Epanodos) of the Ecumenical Patriarch to the patriarchal throne: Arriving at the Patriarchate with his customary entourage, the Patriarch halts before the Great Portal of the cathedral church and vests in the Mandya. The accompanying Hierarchs enter directly into the Holy Sanctuary (Bema). Preceded by the Primikirios carrying the double-candled Divamboulon and four deacons (two of whom cense the Patriarch as he enters), the Choirs chant "It is truly meet to bless thee, O Theotokos...". The Patriarch stands in the midst of the church and imparts a blessing, but without carrying the patriarchal staff. Two Hierarchs escort him to ascend the Patriarchal Cathedra; the Metropolitan of Herakleia approaches, delivers the traditional greeting, bestows the Patriarchal Staff upon him, kisses his right hand, and enters the Sanctuary. The Great Logothete stands at the Parathrone. The Great Ecclesiarch (Grand Master of Ceremonies) invests the Patriarch with Epitrachilion and Omophorion. The Great Archdeacon exclaims "Bless, Master", the Patriarch intones the blessing, the Archdeacon reads the Trisagion Prayers, and the Patriarch gives the Exclamation. The First Choir chants the Dismissal Troparion of Saint George in Tone 4 ("As the liberator of captives..."), and the Second Choir chants the Theotokion ("Open unto us the gate of compassion..."). The Great Archdeacon intones the Ektenia of Fervent Supplication ("Have mercy on us, O God..."), with petitions for all devout Orthodox Christians, for the Archbishop, and for preservation of the city and temple. The Patriarch intones the Exclamation "Hear us, O God our Savior...", the Protopsaltes sings the Patriarchal Polychronion, and the customary allocution is delivered from the Ambo.
[^511]: Ceremony of the Return of a Patriarch: Conclusion and Final Dismissal (§ Return of a Patriarch 2). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p903 (printed page 888) and facing English leaf p904 (printed page 889). Conclusion of the ceremonial of patriarchal return: As the allocution concludes, the Choirs chant the episcopal acclamation "Our Master and Chief Priest, O Lord, preserve for many years!", while the Patriarch imparts his blessing upon the clergy and faithful. From within the Holy Sanctuary (Bema), the clergy chant the concluding prayer: "Through the prayers of our holy Master, Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us and save us. Amen.", concluding the solemn office of the Patriarch's return to the Ecumenical Cathedra.
[^512]: Ceremony of the Indiction in the Great Church on September 1: Assembly, Troparia, and Litany (§ Ceremony of the Indiction 1). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p905 (printed page 890) and facing English leaf p906 (printed page 891). The solemn ecclesiastical order for the beginning of the New Church Year (Indiction) on September 1 in the Ecumenical Patriarchate: On September 1, the Patriarch descends to the patriarchal cathedral church for Vespers, and on the morning of the feast attends Matins and the Divine Liturgy, seated at the Parathrone wearing the monastic Epanokalymmavchion and holding the unadorned staff (Chazranion). Following the Divine Liturgy, the synodal Hierarchs withdraw from the Sanctuary into the Great Synodikon. After the distribution of the Antidoron to the faithful, the Patriarch proceeds in solemn procession to the Great Synodikon, preceded by the processional Exapteryga (seraphic fans), processional torches, the Primikirios bearing the Divamboulon, and the Choirs of Chanters. In the Synodikon, the Patriarch vests in Epitrachilion and Omophorion. The Great Archdeacon exclaims "Bless, Master", the Patriarch gives the blessing "Blessed is our God...", the Archdeacon recites the Trisagion Prayers, and the Patriarch gives the Exclamation. The First Choir chants the Dismissal Troparion of the Indiction in Tone 2 ("O Creator of the universe..."), the Second Choir chants the Dismissal Troparion of Saint Simeon the Stylite in Tone 1 ("Thou hast become a pillar of endurance..."), and the First Choir chants the Theotokion in Tone 1 ("Rejoice, full of grace, Virgin Theotokos..."). The Great Archdeacon and Second Deacon alternate in chanting the petitions of the Fervent Ektenia for all Orthodox Christians, for the Archbishop, for deliverance of the church and city from every calamity, and for the hearing of prayer, answered by threefold responses of "Lord, have mercy" by the Choirs. The Patriarch pronounces the Exclamation "Hear us, O God our Savior...", leading to the Dismissal.
[^513]: Ceremony of the Indiction Concluded: Synodal Act, Signatures, and Hand-Kissing (§ Ceremony of the Indiction 2). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p907 (printed page 892) and facing English leaf p908 (printed page 893). Solemn execution of the Synodal Act of the Indiction: As the Dismissal concludes, the Choirs chant "Our Master and Chief Priest, O Lord, preserve for many years!". The Ecumenical Patriarch reads aloud the official Synodal Act (Praxis) of the Indiction, promulgating the entrance into the new ecclesiastical year and imparting synodal absolution and blessing upon all devout and Orthodox Christians of every rank and degree throughout the world. Following the reading, the Choirs chant the Patriarchal Polychronion. The Patriarch signs the formal registry of the Indiction, followed by all the members of the Holy Synod in order of episcopal seniority, all vested in Epitrachilion and Omophorion. During the signing, the Choirs chant the festal Idiomelon hymns appointed for the Indiction. Following the signing, all hierarchs, clergy, dignitaries, and faithful approach to kiss the right hand of His All-Holiness, concluding the patriarchal rite of the New Year.
[^514]: Horologion of the Great Church for the Beginning and Ending of Services of the Great Feasts (§ Feasts Horologion 1). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p909 (printed page 894) and facing English leaf p910 (printed page 895). Ancient Byzantine horary schedule governing patriarchal celebrations and bell-tolling in the Great Church of Constantinople: The rubric establishes the temporal coordination between the bell-ringing (simantron/peal), the descent of the Patriarch from the patriarchal apartments into the cathedral, and the final Dismissal for the major winter dominical solemnities. The ancient Byzantine system calculates twenty-four hours beginning at sunset (twelve hours of night followed by twelve hours of day). On December 24 (Christmas Eve), when the Royal Hours are conjoined to the Liturgy of Saint Basil, the bell tolls at the 12th Hour of the Night (6:00 a.m.), the Patriarch descends at the 1st Hour of the Day (7:00 a.m.), and the Dismissal occurs at the 4th Hour of the Day (10:00 a.m.); if the Hours are chanted alone (on Saturday or Sunday Paramony), bells toll at the 1st Hour of the Day (7:00 a.m.), the Patriarch descends at the 2nd Hour (8:00 a.m.), and Dismissal occurs at the 4th Hour (10:00 a.m.). On the Nativity of Christ (December 25), Matins bells toll at the 9th Hour of the Night (3:00 a.m.), the Patriarch descends at the 10th Hour and a half of the Night (4:30 a.m.), and the Divine Liturgy ends at the 1st Hour and a half of the Day (7:30 a.m.). On January 1 (Circumcision and Saint Basil), Matins bells toll at the 1st Hour of the Day (7:00 a.m.), the Patriarch descends at the 2nd Hour and a half (8:30 a.m.), and Dismissal occurs at the 4th Hour and a half (10:30 a.m.). On January 5 (Theophany Eve), when Hours are joined to Saint Basil's Liturgy, bells toll at the 11th Hour of the Night (5:00 a.m.), the Patriarch descends at the 12th Hour of the Night (6:00 a.m.), and Dismissal occurs at the 3rd Hour of the Day (9:00 a.m.); if the Hours are chanted alone, bells toll at the 1st Hour of the Day (7:00 a.m.), the Patriarch descends at the 2nd Hour (8:00 a.m.), and Dismissal occurs at the 4th Hour (10:00 a.m.).
[^515]: Constantinopolitan Feasts Horologion Concluded: Theophany, Great Fast, Holy Week, and Pascha (§ Feasts Horologion 2). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p911 (printed page 896) and facing English leaf p912 (printed page 897). The horary schedule of the Ecumenical Patriarchate for bell-tolling and patriarchal descent during the festal cycles of Theophany, Great Lent, Holy Week, and the Paschal Solemnity: On January 6 (Holy Theophany), bells toll at the 10th Hour of the Night (4:00 a.m.), the Patriarch descends at the 11th Hour and a half of the Night (5:30 a.m.), and Dismissal occurs at the 2nd Hour of the Day (8:00 a.m.). On the Sunday of Orthodoxy, bells toll at the 10th Hour and a half of the Night (4:30 a.m.), the Patriarch descends at the 12th Hour of the Night (6:00 a.m.), and Dismissal occurs at the 2nd Hour and a half of the Day (8:30 a.m.). On Holy and Great Thursday, Matins bells toll at the 11th Hour of the Night (5:00 a.m.), the Patriarch descends at the 12th Hour of the Night (6:00 a.m.), and Dismissal occurs at the 1st Hour and a half of the Day (7:30 a.m.); on Thursday evening (Passion Gospels), bells toll at the 12th Hour of the Day (6:00 p.m.), the Patriarch descends at 6:30 p.m., and Dismissal occurs at the 4th Hour of the Night (10:00 p.m.). On Holy and Great Friday, Royal Hours bells toll at the 11th Hour of the Night (5:00 a.m.), the Patriarch descends at 5:30 a.m., and Dismissal occurs at the 3rd Hour and a half of the Day (9:30 a.m.); on Friday evening (Epitaphios / Lamentations), bells toll at the 12th Hour of the Day (6:00 p.m.), the Patriarch descends at 6:30 p.m., and Dismissal occurs at the 4th Hour of the Night (10:00 p.m.). On Holy and Great Saturday morning, bells toll at the 11th Hour of the Night (5:00 a.m.), and Dismissal occurs at the 1st Hour and a half of the Day (7:30 a.m.). On the Sunday of Holy Pascha, bells toll at the 5th Hour of the Night (11:00 p.m.), the Patriarch descends at 11:30 p.m., the Anastasis service begins at the 6th Hour and a half of the Night (12:30 a.m.), and Dismissal occurs at the 9th Hour of the Night (3:00 a.m.). At Vespers of the Resurrection (Agape Vespers), bells toll at the 3rd Hour of the Day (9:00 a.m.), the Patriarch descends at the 4th Hour of the Day (10:00 a.m.), and Dismissal occurs at the 6th Hour of the Day (12:00 Noon). On Bright Monday, Matins bells toll at the 11th Hour of the Night (5:00 a.m.), the Patriarch descends at 5:30 a.m., and Dismissal occurs at the 1st Hour and a half of the Day (7:30 a.m.).
[^516]: Register of Feasts at Which the Patriarch Officiates: Immovable Calendar from September through August (§ Patriarchal Feasts 1). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p913 (printed page 898) and facing English leaf p914 (printed page 899). The official patriarchal directory specifying all stationary feasts throughout the liturgical year wherein the Ecumenical Patriarch is required to officiate (chorostatein) in the Patriarchal Cathedral of Saint George at the Phanar: The general rule mandates patriarchal officiation at all Saturday evening Great Vespers and all Sunday services throughout the entire ecclesiastical year, as well as on the Patriarch's personal patronal feast (namesday). The fixed calendar enumerates twenty-four stationary solemnities: September 1 (The Indiction, observed from the Parathrone), September 8 (Nativity of the Theotokos), September 14 (Exaltation of the Holy Cross), October 26 (Saint Demetrios), November 8 (The Archangels), November 21 (Entrance of the Theotokos), November 30 (Saint Andrew), December 6 (Saint Nicholas), December 24 (Paramony of the Nativity at the Royal Hours), December 25 (Nativity of Christ), December 26 (Synaxis of the Theotokos), January 1 (Circumcision of Christ), January 5 (Paramony of Theophany at the Royal Hours), January 6 (Holy Theophany), January 7 (Synaxis of the Forerunner), January 30 (The Three Hierarchs), February 2 (Meeting of the Lord), March 25 (Annunciation of the Theotokos), April 23 (Saint George), May 21 (Saints Constantine and Helen), June 24 (Nativity of the Forerunner), June 29 (Holy Apostles Peter and Paul), July 11 (Saint Euphemia), and August 6 (Transfiguration of the Lord).
[^517]: Patriarchal Officiations during the Great Fast and Synodal Concelebration Norms (§ Patriarchal Feasts 2). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p915 (printed page 900) and facing English leaf p916 (printed page 901). The seasonal liturgical order for patriarchal services during Holy and Great Lent, Bright Week, and the Pentecostarion, together with the canonical protocols governing synodal concelebration and use of the Parathronion: The fixed list concludes with August 15 (Dormition of the Theotokos) and August 29 (Beheading of the Forerunner). During Great Lent, the Patriarch officiates on Friday evening for the Akathist Hymn and on Saturday morning from the Parathrone; on Holy Thursday morning at the Divine Liturgy and evening at the Twelve Passion Gospels; on Holy Friday at the Royal Hours and Vespers, and evening at the Epitaphios Matins; on Holy Saturday morning at Vespers and Saint Basil's Liturgy; on Easter Sunday, Bright Monday, Bright Tuesday, Bright Friday, and Ascension Thursday. By ancient patriarchal tradition, the members of the Holy Synod concelebrate (synochorostatein) with the Patriarch at all Matins and Divine Liturgies, but only at fifteen specified Great Vespers: the Patriarch's namesday, the Indiction, Christmas Eve, Theophany Eve, New Year's Day, Sunday of Orthodoxy, Akathist Friday, Annunciation, Palm Sunday, Holy Thursday, Holy Friday, Holy Saturday, Paschal Agape Vespers, Saint George, and Thomas Sunday. On non-synodal occasions, the Patriarch attends without Mandya, holding the plain walking staff (chazranion) and seated at the secondary throne (parathrone / Turkish gedeki), including Sunday evening Lenten Vespers, Presanctified Liturgies, Great Compline, the Great Canon, and the Feast of Saint John Chrysostom.
[^518]: Order of the Ordination of a Hierarch: Weekday and Ordinary Sunday Rubrics (§ Episcopal Ordination 1). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p917 (printed page 902) and facing English leaf p918 (printed page 903). Canonical ordinals governing the insertion of Pentecostal pneumatological propers during the consecration of a bishop: Because the fullness of the episcopate participates directly in the apostolic descent of the Holy Spirit, Pentecostal hymns are integrated into the Divine Office and Liturgy. Under Situation 1 (an ordinary weekday without a celebrating saint), the entire service is chanted from Pentecost: Dismissal Troparia, Kontakia, Kathismata, Canons, Katavasiai, Exapostilaria, Praises (Ainoi), Antiphons, Entrance Hymn, Epistle (Acts 2:1–11), Gospel, and Communion Hymn ("Thy Good Spirit..."). Under Situation 2 (an ordinary Sunday), the Resurrection office is intercalated with Pentecost: Katavasiai of Pentecost with Ode 9 after "More honorable"; Exapostilaria of the Resurrection and Pentecost (without Theotokion); Praises with four Resurrection stichera followed by Pentecost stichera ("Glorious things today..."), Glory ("Heavenly King..."), Now and ever ("Most blessed art thou..."), and "Today salvation..."; Liturgy Antiphons of Pentecost; Entrance Hymn with paschal/Sunday refrain; Dismissal Troparia of the Resurrection, Pentecost ("Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God..."), the temple patron, and Kontakion of Pentecost ("When the Most High descended..."); Epistle of Pentecost and Sunday Gospel; Megalynarion "It is truly meet..."; and Communion Hymn "Thy Good Spirit..." (Psalm 142:10). Situation 3 establishes that on a weekday with a commemorating saint, the same proportion is preserved.
[^519]: Order of the Ordination of a Hierarch: Commemorated Saints, Marian Solemnities, and Dominical Feasts (§ Episcopal Ordination 2). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p919 (printed page 904) and facing English leaf p920 (printed page 905). Conclusion of rubrics for episcopal ordination coinciding with festal ranks of the liturgical year: Under Situation 3 (concluded), on a weekday with a commemorated saint, the hymns of Pentecost indisputably precede those of the saint. Under Situation 4 (a Sunday with a Polyeleos or Great Doxology saint), Pentecost Katavasiai and Ode 9 are sung; Exapostilaria of the Resurrection, Pentecost, the Saint, and Theotokion; Praises with two Resurrection stichera, three of Pentecost, and three of the Saint, Glory ("Heavenly King..."), Now and ever ("Most blessed..."); Liturgy Antiphons of Pentecost, and Troparia of the Resurrection, Pentecost, Saint of the day, temple patron, and Kontakion of Pentecost. Under Situation 5 (a Feast of the Theotokos), the seasonal Katavasiai are retained, the Exapostilarion of Pentecost precedes that of the Marian feast, two Pentecost stichera are chanted at Praises with Glory ("Heavenly King..."), the Marian festal Antiphons are sung at Liturgy, and after the Entrance the Dismissal Troparion of Pentecost precedes that of the Theotokos, with the Epistle of Pentecost. Under Situation 6 (a Great Dominical Feast of the Lord, misnumbered as Situation 7 in the Greek editio princeps), the supreme priority of the Lord's feast is maintained: only the Exapostilarion of Pentecost is admitted (framed before and after by that of the Dominical Feast), and after the Entrance only the Dismissal Troparion of Pentecost is intoned from within the Holy Sanctuary following that of the Feast, admitting no further Pentecostal propers.
[^520]: Physical leaf p922 (printed page 907), rubric on concelebration of multiple hierarchs. The text outlines liturgical precedence: the senior hierarch presides from the cathedra wearing the Mandya, while concelebrating hierarchs enter the sanctuary directly to vest, receiving the episcopal blessing from the senior hierarch before beginning the vesting prayers.
[^521]: Physical leaf p924 (printed page 909), rubric on communion of concelebrating hierarchs and distribution of exclamations. In Constantinople practice as codified by Violakis, the presiding hierarch blesses, consecrates the Holy Gifts, and commemorates during the Great Entrance. Concelebrating hierarchs self-commune of the Holy Bread and Holy Chalice, while concelebrating priests and deacons receive the Holy Mysteries from the hands of the hierarch lowest in episcopal seniority (*ὁ τῇ τάξει ἔσχατος τῶν συλλειτουργούντων Ἀρχιερέων*).
[^522]: Physical leaf p926 (printed page 911), start of the Order of the Burial of Priests (*Τάξις εἰς κεκοιμημένον Ἱερέα*). The service commences at the deceased priest's house with the Trisagion prayers and Psalm 90, followed by the solemn procession to the temple, during which the clergy and chanters chant the slow funeral Troparia in Tone 2 (*Ὁ βάθει σοφίας*).
[^523]: Physical leaf p928 (printed page 913), the First and Second Stases of the Amomos (Psalm 118 [LXX 118, MT 119]) at the Funeral of a Priest. The refrains alternate: in the First Stasis (*Ἄμωμοι ἐν ὁδῷ*), the refrain is *Ἀλληλούϊα* (Alleluia); in the Second Stasis (*Αἱ χεῖρές σου ἐποίησάν με*), the refrain is *Ἐλέησόν με, Κύριε* (Have mercy on me, Lord). Each stasis concludes with a Little Ektenia for the departed.
[^524]: Physical leaf p930 (printed page 915), the Third Stasis of the Amomos (*Καὶ ἐλέησόν με*) with refrain *Ἀλληλούϊα*, followed immediately by the Funeral Evlogitaria in Tone 5 (*Εὐλογητὸς εἶ, Κύριε, δίδαξόν με τὰ δικαιώματά σου* / *Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thy statutes*). The final Theotokion *Χαῖρε σεμνὴ* is chanted before the Small Ektenia for the deceased and the funeral Sessional Hymn in Tone 5.
[^525]: Physical leaf p932 (printed page 917), rubrics governing the concelebration of multiple presbyters during the Funeral Evlogitaria and the beginning of the Eight Idiomela of Saint John of Damascus (Tones 1–4). In Constantinopolitan practice, the deacon intones a petition after each troparion of the Evlogitaria, and each concelebrating priest takes turns pronouncing the exclamation according to seniority of ordination. The idiomela that follow constitute the classical Byzantine hymnographic meditation on mortality, vanity, and resurrection.
[^526]: Physical leaf p934 (printed page 919), concluding strophes of the Funeral Idiomela of Saint John of Damascus (Tones 5 through 8) and the Doxastikon in Tone 8 (*Ὁ θάνατός σου, Κύριε*). The hymns integrate biblical citations from the Prophets (Job 30:19; Genesis 18:27: *“I am but dust and ashes”*) with patristic theology regarding the composite constitution of human nature and the despoiling of Hades through the death and burial of Christ.
[^527]: Physical leaf p936 (printed page 921), rubrics for the Funeral Absolution prayers, the Paschal Funeral during Bright Week (Renewal Week), and the universal application of the funeral rite across all ecclesiastical and social orders. During Bright Week, mourning is superseded by the Paschal Canon and Paschal Stichera (*“It is the day of Resurrection”*). For deceased infants, the Damascene idiomela are omitted and replaced by the special euchological prayers for infants.
[^528]: Physical leaf p938 (printed page 923), mystical theology of Memorial Services (*Mnemosyna*) according to Saint Symeon of Thessalonica (3rd, 9th, 40th days, and 3-, 6-, 9-month commemorations), followed by the solemn colophon marking the conclusion of the 1888 Violakis Typikon text (*“ΤΕΛΟΣ ΚΑΙ Τῼ ΘΕῼ ΔΟΞΑ”* / *“The End and to God be Glory”*). Commemorations on the 3rd day honor the Holy Trinity; the 9th day invokes the nine angelic orders; the 40th day points to the Ascension and the General Resurrection.
[^529]: Physical leaf p940 (printed page 925), editorial introduction by Demos and Bogdanos to the perpetual Paschalion and Table of Feasts (*Πίναξ τοῦ Ἑορτοδρομίου*). The original 1888 edition of George Violakis calculated all movable feasts, Pascha, and fasting periods according to the Julian Calendar. Following the calendar reform of the 1920s, twentieth-century editors provided dual concordance dates for both the Julian Calendar (JC) and Gregorian Calendar (GC) to preserve the functional utility of the Typikon tables.
[^530]: Physical leaf p942 (printed page 927), editorial commentary and comparative outline of movable feasts under the Gregorian Calendar (GC) alongside the Julian Calendar (JC) foundation of the 1888 Violakis Typikon. Because the Gregorian Calendar advances 13 days ahead of the Julian reckoning in the 20th and 21st centuries, the Triodion commences 13 days later (January 24 GC earliest versus January 11 JC), causing the earliest immovable feasts of January (St. Theodosios the Cenobiarch on Jan 11, St. Anthony the Great on Jan 17, Sts. Athanasius and Cyril on Jan 18, and St. Euthymius the Great on Jan 20) to fall outside the Triodion period under the Revised Julian / Gregorian reckoning. Conversely, the extended terminus of Pentecost delays the Sunday of All Saints up to July 3 GC, thereby allowing mid-summer immovable feasts (the Nativity of Saint John the Baptist on June 24, the Holy Apostles Peter and Paul on June 29, and the Synaxis of the Twelve Apostles on June 30) to coincide with the post-Paschal cycle.
[^531]: Physical leaf p944 (printed page 929), preamble and title of the Interpretive Rules (*Ἑρμηνευτικοὶ τούτου κανόνες*) for the perpetual Table of Feasts (*Πίναξ τοῦ Ἑορτοδρομίου*). The table establishes a systematic liturgical concordance resolving coincidences between the immovable feasts of the Menologion and the movable cycles of the Triodion and Pentecostarion. The mathematical horizon of the table is strictly bounded by the earliest possible start of the Triodion on January 11 JC (January 24 GC) and the latest possible termination of the Pentecostarion on May 25 JC (July 3 GC, the Sunday of All Saints), ensuring comprehensive rubrication for all eighteen intersecting immovable commemorations.
[^532]: Physical leaf p946 (printed page 931), Interpretive Rules 1, 2, and 3 governing the 34-day span of Paschal oscillation (March 22 to April 25 JC; April 4 to May 8 GC) and its impact on the 18 intersecting immovable feasts of the Menologion. Twelve of these feasts occur within the Triodion period (from Saint Theodosios on January 11 through the Annunciation on March 25 and early May commemorations), while six occur within the Pentecostarion period (up through the Third Finding of the Head of the Forerunner on May 25 JC). The rule instructs typikaris and chanters how to cross-reference the opening date of the Triodion with the Paschal date in columns 1 and 2 to locate the exact day of coincidence and the corresponding rubrical rubric paragraph in the body of the Violakis Typikon.
[^533]: Physical leaf p948 (printed page 933), Interpretive Rule 4 (first part) addressing the calendar discrepancy caused by bissextile intercalation (Leap Years) occurring prior to the leap day of February 29. In a common (non-leap) year, the temporal distance between the beginning of the Triodion (Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee) and Holy Pascha is exactly 70 days. In a leap year, the insertion of February 29 introduces an additional day into February, shifting the beginning of the Triodion by one calendar date relative to the Paschal date (e.g., in 1889 Pascha fell on April 9 with the Triodion opening on January 29, whereas in the leap year 1900 with Pascha likewise on April 9, the Triodion opened on January 30). For immovable feasts falling prior to February 29, the rubrist must shift down one row in the table to obtain the correct day of the week.
[^534]: Physical leaf p950 (printed page 935), conclusion of Interpretive Rule 4 and Rule 5. The text demonstrates the bissextile principle using two benchmark commemorations: the Presentation of our Lord (February 2, prior to leap day, which shifts from Thursday in non-leap 1889 to Wednesday in leap 1900) versus the Annunciation of the Theotokos (March 25, subsequent to leap day, which remains invariant on Saturday of the Fifth Week of Great Lent in both years). Rule 5 defines the extreme boundary condition: when Holy Pascha falls on its latest possible date of April 24 JC (May 7 GC) in a leap year, the Triodion commences on February 14 JC (February 27 GC) rather than February 13 JC (February 26 GC).
[^535]: Physical leaf p952 (printed page 937), title leaf and explanatory instructions for the Chart of Feasts (*Ο Εορτοδρόμιος Πίναξ* / *The Chart of the Feasts*). The instructions specify that the first date listed in columns 1 and 2 represents the Julian Calendar reckoning (JC / ΙΗ), while the date in parentheses indicates the corresponding civil Gregorian Calendar date (GC / ΓΗ) calculated by adding 13 days (the solar divergence between the calendars during the 20th and 21st centuries). Column 4 references cross-reference the corresponding rubrical paragraphs (§) and pagination of George Violakis's *Typikon of the Great Church of Christ* (specifically referencing the third edition published by V. Saliveros, Athens, c. 1950–1960).
[^536]: Physical leaf p954 (printed page 939), opening section of the perpetual Table of Feasts detailing the movable concurrences for January 11 (Venerable Father Theodosios the Cenobiarch), January 17 (Venerable Father Anthony the Great), and January 18 (Our Holy Fathers Athanasios and Cyril of Alexandria, part 1). For Saint Theodosios (the earliest possible opening of the Triodion), the table documents the solitary occurrence when Pascha falls on March 22 JC (April 4 GC) and the Triodion opens on January 11 JC, placing the saint's feast on the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee (§§ 42–44, p. 155). For Saint Anthony the Great, the feast shifts across the days of the Week of the Publican and the Pharisee as Pascha advances from March 22 to March 28 JC, culminating on the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee itself when Pascha falls on March 28 JC (§§ 5–6, pp. 158–159).
[^537]: Physical leaf p956 (printed page 941), continuation of the Table of Feasts covering January 18 (Athanasios and Cyril, conclusion), January 20 (Venerable Father Euthymios the Great), and January 25 (Our Father among the Saints Gregory the Theologian, part 1). The facing printed English edition on p. 941 exhibits multiple severe typographical discrepancies in the conversion numerals of the first table: the Julian/Gregorian dates for the opening of the Triodion were misprinted as "13 (20)" through "18 (25)" instead of the correct 13-day advance "13 (26)" through "18 (31)", and Paschal dates were misprinted as "24 (31)" through "29 (5 April)" instead of the correct Julian dates "24 (6 April)" through "29 (11 April)". Furthermore, under January 20, the Gregorian dates for January 19 and 20 JC were erroneously printed as "1 April" and "2 April" instead of 1 and 2 February. These epigraphic defects have been systematically rectified in the critical translation text.
[^538]: Physical leaf p958 (printed page 943), continuation of the Table of Feasts covering January 25 (Gregory the Theologian, conclusion) and January 27 (Translation of the Relics of Saint John Chrysostom, part 1). The table traces the migration of Saint Gregory the Theologian's commemoration from Meatfare Sunday through the Week of the Prodigal Son and into the Week of the Publican and the Pharisee as Pascha advances into April. For Saint John Chrysostom (January 27), the feast intersects with Cheesefare Tuesday when Pascha occurs on March 22 JC (April 4 GC), Meatfare Sunday when Pascha falls on March 24 JC (§§ 5–6, p. 165), the Saturday of Souls when Pascha falls on March 25 JC (§ 7, p. 165), and the Sunday of the Prodigal Son when Pascha falls on March 31 JC (§§ 5–6, p. 165).
[^539]: Physical leaf p960 (printed page 945), conclusion of the concurrence table for January 27 (John Chrysostom) and opening of January 30 (The Three Hierarchs: Basil the Great, Gregory the Theologian, and John Chrysostom). When Pascha falls on March 27 JC, the commemoration of the Three Hierarchs coincides with Meatfare Sunday (§§ 5–7, pp. 167–168; note that the printed English edition erroneously lists "27 (9 March)" instead of 9 April for the Paschal date). When Pascha falls on March 28 JC, the feast coincides with the Saturday of Souls (§ 8, p. 168), demanding the solemn synthesis of the hierarches' festal hymns with the general requiem propers for all departed souls from all ages.
[^540]: Physical leaf p962 (printed page 947), continuation of the perpetual Table of Feasts covering January 30 (The Three Hierarchs, conclusion) and February 2 (The Presentation of Our Lord Jesus Christ into the Temple, part 1). For the Three Hierarchs, the table completes the cycle as Pascha advances into April, concluding on the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee when Pascha falls on April 10 JC (§§ 5–7, pp. 167–168). For the Presentation of Our Lord, the commemoration coincides with Clean Monday (the first day of the Great Fast) when Pascha falls on its earliest possible date of March 22 JC (§§ 21–24, pp. 180–181), and with Cheesefare Sunday when Pascha falls on March 23 JC (§§ 11, 13, 20, pp. 175–179). The printed English edition on p. 947 exhibits typographical errors in Column 1 ("29 (9)" and "30 (10)" for 29 and 30 January) and shifts the Column 4 rubrical references upward by one row beginning at January 18. These defects are corrected in the critical translation.
[^541]: Physical leaf p964 (printed page 949), conclusion of the concurrence table for February 2 (The Presentation of Our Lord into the Temple) and opening of February 10 (The Holy Hieromartyr Charalambos, part 1). For Saint Charalambos, the table traces the feast across the First and Second Weeks of the Great Fast. When Pascha occurs on March 22 JC (April 4 GC), Charalambos coincides with Tuesday of the Second Week of Lent (§ 28, p. 190); when Pascha falls on March 24 JC, the feast coincides with the Sunday of the Triumph of Orthodoxy (§§ 23–25, pp. 188–189); and when Pascha falls on March 25 JC, the saint's office coincides with the Saturday of Saint Theodore the Recruit (§§ 20–22, pp. 187–188).
[^542]: Physical leaf p966 (printed page 951), continuation of the Table of Feasts covering February 10 (Saint Charalambos, conclusion) and February 24 (The First and Second Finding of the Venerable Head of the Holy Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John, part 1). The facing pages 950–951 in the printed edition exhibit a systematic typographical aberration in Column 1: the compositor miscalculated the Julian-to-Gregorian divergence as 16 days instead of 13, printing "21 (6)" through "31 (16)" instead of 21 (3 February) through 31 (13 February). Furthermore, the page numbers cited in Column 4 of the Greek text erroneously cite pages 172–175 for the pre-Lenten Sundays instead of pages 182–183. [^543]: Physical leaf p968 (printed page 953), continuation of the Table of Feasts for February 24 (Finding of the Head of the Forerunner, part 2). For Saint George (April 23), the feast falls during the Paschal season (Pentecostarion): Thursday of the Fifth Week of the Samaritan Woman (§§ 35–37, pp. 250–251) when Pascha falls on its earliest date of March 22 JC, Wednesday of the Apodosis of Mid-Pentecost (§ 34, p. 250), the Sunday of the Samaritan Woman (§ 33, p. 250), Saturday of the Fourth Week of the Paralytic (§§ 30–32, p. 249), Wednesday of Mid-Pentecost (§§ 27–29, pp. 248–249), and the Sunday of the Paralytic (§§ 23–25, pp. 247–248). The printed English edition displays minor typographical lapses ("1-239" for § 1, p. 239).

[^557]: Physical leaf p996 (printed page [981] / TD-2), opening the *Introduction to This Dictionary* of the extensive scholarly appendix *Dictionary of Terms Related to the Typikon* compiled, adapted, and edited by George T. Demos, MD, for the 2007 Athens/Denver bilingual publication of the 1888 Violakis Typikon. While the 1888 Greek original printed in Constantinople ends with the Eortodromion tables on page 978 (physical leaf p993), this bilingual critical edition incorporates Demos's lexicon (pp. TD-1 to TD-112) as an integral reference monument. Demos outlines the scope of the dictionary, acknowledging its primary foundation in the 1888 Violakis Typikon and Theodore Bogdanos's 1993 study *The Byzantine Liturgy: Hymnology and Order*.
[^558]: Physical leaf p998 (printed page [983] / TD-4), concluding the preliminary matter of the *Dictionary of Terms Related to the Typikon* with Dr. George T. Demos's acknowledgements to the hierarchs, clergy, and liturgical consultants of the Greek Orthodox Metropolis of Denver, specifically noting Rev. Protopresbyter Robert T. Athas, translator of the English text of the 1888 Violakis Typikon, and Theodore Bogdanos, Professor Emeritus of San Jose State University.

In this critical edition, entries designating Greek liturgical terms are harmonized with the Master Translation Standard (MTS-1) and the Ecosystem Liturgical Lexicon, rectifying non-canonical terminology such as *Apolytikia* to "Dismissal Troparia" and aligning modal designations with the Ruthenian Tone system (Tones 1–8).

Psalter citations (e.g. Psalms 50, 103, 118, 119) are anchored in the canonical Septuagint (LXX) numbering with corresponding Masoretic (MT) references supplied in brackets.
